For applications and a discussion of related results see also
[
20
,
48
,
90
,
91
,
119
]
.

3.
Stratified Morse theory for constructible sheaves

In this section, we explain the basic results from
[
113
]
about
stratified Morse theory

in the framework of (weakly) constructible sheaves in the
complex context
, continuing and extending the recent survey of Goresky
[
49
]
in this handbook series (as well as the survey of Massey
[
88
]
).
We follow the notions of the geometric
stratified Morse theory
of
Goresky-MacPherson so that one can easily compare our results with
those of
[
47
]
. Moreover, we also explain some relations to the general
micro-local sheaf theory

of Kashiwara-Schapira
[
67
]
. But unlike most of these references, we do not need a
global embedding
into an ambient complex manifold
(except for the index theorems for characteristic cycles of constructible sheaves and the comparison with the notion of micro-support from
[
67
]
).

3.1.
Morse functions, local and normal Morse data

We work with a fixed complex algebraic (or analytic) variety
X
with a given Whitney stratification
{\mathscr{S}}
.

Definition 3.1
.

A function
f\colon X\to{\mathbb{R}}
is a
C^{k}
-differentiable function (with
2\leq k\leq\infty
)
if, for any
x\in X
, there exists a local embedding
(X,x)\hookrightarrow({\mathbb{C}}^{n},x)
such that
f
is induced by restriction of a

C^{k}
-function germ
\hat{f}\colon({\mathbb{C}}^{n},x)\to({\mathbb{R}},f(x))
.

Of course, a complex algebraic (or analytic) function or morphism
h\colon X\to{\mathbb{C}}
is by definition locally given by restriction of a
complex algebraic (or analytic) function germ
\hat{h}\colon({\mathbb{C}}^{n},x)\to({\mathbb{C}},h(x))
.

Definition 3.2
.

A point
x\in X
is called a
stratified critical point
of
f\colon X\to{\mathbb{R}}
, resp.,
h\colon X\to{\mathbb{C}}
(with respect to
{\mathscr{S}}
) if

f\colon S\to{\mathbb{R}}
, resp.,
h\colon S\to{\mathbb{C}}
is not a submersion at the point
x
belonging to the stratum
S\in{\mathscr{S}}
.
Let
\mathrm{Sing}(f|_{S})
, resp.,
\mathrm{Sing}(h|_{S})
be the set of critical points of
f
, resp.,
h
in the stratum
S\in{\mathscr{S}}
, with

\mathrm{Sing}_{{\mathscr{S}}}(f):=\bigcup_{S\in{\mathscr{S}}}\mathrm{Sing}(f|_{S})\quad\text{resp.,}\quad\mathrm{Sing}_{{\mathscr{S}}}(h):=\bigcup_{S\in{\mathscr{S}}}\mathrm{Sing}(h|_{S})\>.

Then
\mathrm{Sing}_{{\mathscr{S}}}(f)
(resp.,
\mathrm{Sing}_{{\mathscr{S}}}(h)
) is a closed (complex algebraic or analytic) subset of
X
by the
Whitney a-condition
.

The following result shows that for
{\mathscr{S}}
-weakly constructible sheaf complexes the local change of cohomology with respect to
f
is located at
the critical points
x\in\mathrm{Sing}_{{\mathscr{S}}}(f)
(see, e.g.,
[
113
, Example 5.1.1]
).

Lemma 3.3
.

Let
f\colon X\to\,]c,d[\,\subset{\mathbb{R}}
be a proper differentiable function, and assume
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{{\mathscr{S}}-wc}(X;R)
is a
{\mathscr{S}}
-weakly constructible complex.

(1)

If
f
is a stratified submersion at a point
x\in\{f=e\}
(with
e\in\,]c,d[\,
, and here
f
does not need to be proper), then

\bigl(R\Gamma_{\{f\geq e\}}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\bigr)_{x}\simeq 0\simeq\bigl(R\Gamma_{\{f\leq e\}}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\bigr)_{x}\>.

(2)

If
f
is a stratified submersion at all points of
\{f=e\}
(for
e\in\,]c,d[\,
), then

R\Gamma(\{f=e\},R\Gamma_{\{f\geq e\}}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}))\simeq 0\simeq R\Gamma(\{f=e\},R\Gamma_{\{f\leq e\}}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}))\>.

(3)

If
f
is a stratified submersion at all points of
\{a<f<b\}
(with
]a,b[\,\subset\,]c,d[\,
), then all cohomology sheaves of
(Rf_{*}{{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}})|_{\,]a,b[\,}
are locally constant.

These results can be proved by induction on the dimension of
X

without using the “first isotopy Lemma of Thom”
. Property 1. above just means that a Whitney b-regular stratification satisfies the
“local stratified acyclicity”
property form
[
113
, Definition 4.0.3]
. In the embedded context of a global closed embedding
k\colon X\hookrightarrow M
into a complex manifold it also implies the following estimate
[
113
, Corollary 4.0.3]
of the

micro-support
of
Rk_{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}
in the sense of Kashiwara-Schapira
[
67
, Definition 5.1.2]
(with
Rk_{*}=k_{*}=k_{!}
the extension by zero):

(20)

\mu supp(Rk_{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\subset\bigcup_{S\in{\mathscr{S}}}\>T^{*}_{S}M\hookrightarrow T^{*}M\quad\text{for any ${\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{{\mathscr{S}}-wc}(X;R)$.}

Here,
\mu supp({\mathscr{G}}^{\bullet})\subset T^{*}M
is by definition the complement of the largest open subset
U\subset T^{*}M
such that

R\Gamma_{\{f\geq f(x)\}}({\mathscr{G}}^{\bullet})_{x}\simeq 0
for any differentiable function germ
f\colon(M,x)\to({\mathbb{R}},f(x))
with
df_{x}\in U
(see
[
67
, Definition 5.1.2]
).
We also use the notation

T^{*}_{S}M
for the
conormal bundle

of the stratum
S
in
M
,
i.e., for the kernel of the natural vector bundle epimorphism
T^{*}M|_{S}\to T^{*}S

dual to the inclusion
TS\hookrightarrow TM|_{S}
. Let us denote by
T^{*}_{{\mathscr{S}}}M
this union of conormal spaces of strata
S\in{\mathscr{S}}
. Then the Whitney a-condition for
{\mathscr{S}}
is equivalent to the fact that
T^{*}_{{\mathscr{S}}}M\hookrightarrow T^{*}M
is closed in
T^{*}M
. Moreover it is
conic
in the sense that it is invariant under the natural
{\mathbb{C}}^{*}
-action on the cotangent bundle. The inclusion of the micro-support in (
20
) implies that

\mu supp(Rk_{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\subset T^{*}M
is a closed complex algebraic (or analytic) conic Lagrangian subset of
T^{*}M
,
and (
20
) is even equivalent to

{\mathscr{S}}
-weak constructibility (see, e.g.,
[
113
, Lemma 4.1]
).

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
{\mathscr{G}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}(M;R)
be given with
\mu supp({\mathscr{G}}^{\bullet})\subset T^{*}_{{\mathscr{S}}}M
. Then
supp({\mathscr{G}}^{\bullet})\subset X
and
{\mathscr{G}}^{\bullet}|_{X}
is
{\mathscr{S}}
-weakly constructible.

Example 3.5
.

Assume
X=M
with
{\mathscr{G}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}(M;R)
be given. Then
\mu supp({\mathscr{G}}^{\bullet})\subset T^{*}_{M}M
the zero section of
T^{*}M
if and only if all cohomology sheaves

{\mathscr{H}}^{i}({\mathscr{G}}^{\bullet})
are locally constant on
M
.

This Example can be proved directly with the
“non-characteristic deformation Lemma”
of Kashiwara (see, e.g.,
[
113
, Proposition 4.1.1]
).
Moreover, it can be used as a substitute for the
“first isotopy Lemma of Thom”
.

If one does not want to fix a complex Whitney stratification
{\mathscr{S}}
, then one gets the following micro-local characterization of weak constructibility
(see, e.g.,
[
113
, Theorem 4.0.1]
and
[
67
, Theorem 8.5.5]
).

Theorem 3.6
.

Let
M
be a complex algebraic (or analytic) manifold. Then
{\mathscr{G}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}(M;R)
is a weakly constructible complex
{\mathscr{G}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{wc}(M;R)
in the complex algebraic (or analytic) sense if and only if
\mu supp({\mathscr{G}}^{\bullet})\subset T^{*}M
is a closed complex algebraic (or analytic) conic Lagrangian subset of
T^{*}M
.

Let us now come back to the general non-embedded context with
{\mathscr{S}}
a fixed Whitney stratification of
X
. Then Lemma
3.3
implies
(see, e.g.,
[
113
, Corollary 5.1.1]
).

Corollary 3.7
.

Let
f\colon X\to\,]c,d[\,\subset{\mathbb{R}}
be a proper differentiable function, and assume
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{{\mathscr{S}}-wc}(X;R)
is a
{\mathscr{S}}
-weakly constructible complex.
If
f
is a stratified submersion at all points of
\{a\leq f\leq b\}\cap\{f\neq e\}
(with
[a,b]\subset\,]c,d[\,
and
e\in\,]a,b[\,
), then one has
distinguished triangles

(21)

R\Gamma(\{f=e\},R\Gamma_{\{f\geq e\}}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}))\to R\Gamma(\{f\leq b\},{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\to R\Gamma(\{f\leq a\},{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\to\>,

and

(22)

R\Gamma_{c}(\{f<a\},{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\to R\Gamma_{c}(\{f<b\},{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\to R\Gamma(\{f=e\},R\Gamma_{\{f\leq e\}}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}))\to\>.

Here,
R\Gamma(\{f=e\},R\Gamma_{\{f\geq e\}}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}))
is the cohomological
counterpart of the
“coarse Morse datum”
as defined in
[
47
, p.62, Definition 3.4]
.
In particular,

R\Gamma(\{f=e\},R\Gamma_{\{f\leq e\}}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\simeq R\Gamma(\{-f=-e\},R\Gamma_{\{-f\geq-e\}}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}))

is the “coarse Morse datum” of
-f
. So this “coarse Morse datum” of
-f
corresponds to the
“relative cohomology with compact support” in the triangle (
22
).
But the cohomology with compact support is in some sense
“dual” to the cohomology with closed support. So this reflects the “duality”
observed in
[
47
, p.27, 2.7]
. In the context of stratified Morse theory
for constructible sheaves, this is a special form of
Poincaré-Verdier duality

(see Equation (
25
)).

The next step is to consider a differentiable function
f
with
isolated
stratified critical
points in
\{f=e\}
, and to localize the “coarse Morse datum” at these
critical points. In terms of sheaf theory, this is quite easy (since
R\Gamma_{\{f\geq e\}}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})|_{\{f=e\}}
is supported on the stratified critical points).

Lemma 3.8
.

Let
f\colon X\to\,]c,d[\,\subset{\mathbb{R}}
be a proper differentiable function, and assume
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{{\mathscr{S}}-wc}(X;R)
is a
{\mathscr{S}}
-weakly constructible complex.
Assume
f
is a stratified submersion at all points of
\{a\leq f\leq b\}
except for finitely many
x_{i}\in\{f=e\}

(with
[a,b]\subset\,]c,d[\,
and
e\in\,]a,b[\,
). Then

(23)

R\Gamma(\{f=e\},R\Gamma_{\{f\geq e\}}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}))\simeq\bigoplus_{i}\>\left(R\Gamma_{\{f\geq e\}}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\right)_{x_{i}}\>.

The stalk complex

(24)

LMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},f,x):=\bigl(R\Gamma_{\{f\geq f(x)\}}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\bigr)_{x}

is the sheaf theoretic counterpart of the
“local Morse datum”

of
[
47
, p.63, Definition 3.5.2]
. The “duality” between
f
and
-f
discussed above is closely related to
Verdier duality for the local Morse data
in the following form of
[
113
, Equation (5.54) on p.314]
:

(25)

{\mathscr{D}}(LMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},-f,x))\simeq LMD({\mathscr{D}}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}),f,x)\quad\text{for ${\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{{\mathscr{S}}-wc}(X;R)$.}

Let us now recall two notions from
[
47
]
.

Definition 3.9
.

Fix a point
x
in the stratum
S\in{\mathscr{S}}
.

(1)

x
is called a
stratified Morse critical point

of the differentiable function germ
f\colon(X,x)\to{\mathbb{R}}
(with respect to

{\mathscr{S}}
), if
f|_{S}
has at
x
a Morse critical point in the
classical sense (i.e., its Hessian at
x
is non-degenerate), and
f=\hat{f}|_{X}
is induced
in some local embedding
(X,x)\hookrightarrow({\mathbb{C}}^{n},x)
by a differentiable function germ
\hat{f}\colon({\mathbb{C}}^{n},x)\to({\mathbb{R}},f(x))

such that the
covector
d\hat{f}_{x}
is
non-degenerate

in the sense of
[
47
, p.44, Definition 1.8]
,
i.e., it does not vanish on any generalized tangent space

\tau:=\lim_{x_{n}\to x}\;T_{x_{n}}S^{\prime}\quad\text{at $x$,}

with
x_{n}
a sequence in a stratum
S^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{S}}
with
S\subset\partial S^{\prime}
.

(2)

A
normal slice

N
at
x\in S\hookrightarrow X\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{n}

is a closed complex submanifold germ
N
of
({\mathbb{C}}^{n},x)
,
with
N\cap S=\{x\}
such that
N
intersects
S

transversally
at
x
(so that
\hbox{\rm codim}\,N=\dim S
).

Note that a
normal slice

N
intersects all strata transversally near
x
by

Whitney a-regularity
. Therefore
N\cap X
gets an induced Whitney
stratification
{\mathscr{S}}|_{N}
near
x
with strata the connected components of intersections
S^{\prime}\cap N\neq\emptyset
(
S^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{S}}
) and
\{x\}=S\cap N
now a point stratum.

Similarly, a
stratified Morse critical point
of
f
is an
isolated stratified critical point of
f
. Finally, if
f=\hat{f}|_{X}
is induced
in some local embedding
(X,x)\hookrightarrow({\mathbb{C}}^{n},x)
by a differentiable function germ
\hat{f}\colon({\mathbb{C}}^{n},x)\to({\mathbb{R}},f(x))

such that the
covector
d\hat{f}_{x}
is
non-degenerate
, then one can find such an
\hat{f}
in any local embedding
(X,x)\hookrightarrow({\mathbb{C}}^{m},x)
.

Example 3.10
.

Let
S
be an open stratum of
X
. Then
x\in S
is a stratified Morse critical point of
f
if and only if
f|_{S}
has at
x
a classical Morse critical point.
If
S=\{x\}
is a point stratum, then
x
is by definition a stratified critical point for any differentiable function
f\colon X\to{\mathbb{R}}
. It is
stratified Morse critical point if and only if
f=\hat{f}|_{X}
is induced
in some local embedding
(X,x)\hookrightarrow({\mathbb{C}}^{n},x)
by a differentiable function germ
\hat{f}\colon({\mathbb{C}}^{n},x)\to({\mathbb{R}},f(x))

such that the covector
d\hat{f}_{x}
is non-degenerate.

Example 3.11
.

Let
X=M
be a complex manifold, with
M^{\prime}\hookrightarrow M
a closed complex submanifold of positive codimension and the Whitney stratification
{\mathscr{S}}
given by the connected components of
M^{\prime}
and
M\backslash M^{\prime}
. Then a differentiable function
f\colon M\to{\mathbb{R}}
has a stratified Morse critical point at
x\in M^{\prime}
if and only if
x
is not a critical point of
f\colon M\to{\mathbb{R}}
(this is equivalent to
df_{x}
is nondegenrate) and
x\in M^{\prime}
is a classical Morse critical point for
f\colon M^{\prime}\to{\mathbb{R}}
.

Let us now state the first main result from stratified Morse theory for constructible sheaves (see,
e.g.,
[
113
, Theorem 5.0.1]
).

Theorem 3.12
.

Let
X
be a complex algebraic (or analytic) variety with a Whitney stratification
{\mathscr{S}}
, and let
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{{\mathscr{S}}-wc}(X;R)
be a given

{\mathscr{S}}
-weakly constructible complex.
Let
f\colon(X,x)\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a differentiable function germ with a stratified Morse critical
point at
x\in S
for some stratum
S\in{\mathscr{S}}
. Take a normal slice
N\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{n}
at
x

in some local embedding
(X,x)\hookrightarrow({\mathbb{C}}^{n},x)
.

(1)

Then one has an isomorphism

\bigl(R\Gamma_{\{f\geq f(x)\}}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\bigr)_{x}\simeq\bigl(R\Gamma_{\{f|_{N\cap X}\geq f(x)\}}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}|_{N\cap X})\bigr)_{x}[-\tau]\>,

with
\tau
the
Morse index of
f|_{S}
(i.e., its Hessian at
x
has exactly

\tau
negative eigenvalues).

(2)

The isomorphism class of

\bigl(R\Gamma_{\{f|_{N\cap X}\geq f(x)\}}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}|_{N\cap X})\bigr)_{x}

is independent of the choice of such a local embedding and normal slice
N
. Moreover,
this isomorphism class only depends on the stratum
S\in{\mathscr{S}}
, but not on the point
x\in S
or the function germ
f=\hat{f}|_{X}
with

d\hat{f}_{x}
non-degenerate in such a local embedding.

The isomorphism class of

(26)

NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},S):=\bigl(R\Gamma_{\{f|_{N\cap X}\geq f(x)\}}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}|_{N\cap X})\bigr)_{x}

is the sheaf theoretic counterpart of the
“normal Morse data”
of

[
47
, p.65, Definition 3.6.1]
. In the (locally) embedded context
k\colon X\hookrightarrow M
,
NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},S)
is also isomorphic to the

“micro-local type of
Rk_{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}
at the non-degenerate covector
\omega=d\hat{f}_{x}
”
in the sense of Kashiwara-Schapira
[
67
, Proposition 6.6.1(ii)]

(as shown in
[
113
, Equation (5.52) on p.311]
).
Part 1. of Theorem
3.12
corresponds to

[
47
, p.8, Theorem SMT Part B; p.65, Main Theorem 3.7]
, and part 2. to

[
47
, p.93, Theorem 7.5.1]
(compare also with
[
47
, p.223, Proposition 6.A.1]
).

Let us consider again the embedded context with
k\colon X\hookrightarrow M
a closed embedding into a complex algebraic (or analytic) manifold,
and
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{{\mathscr{S}}-wc}(X;R)
.
By using the estimate (
20
) and the
involutivity
of
\mu supp(Rk_{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})

[
67
, Theorem 6.5.4]
one can show that
\mu supp(Rk_{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})
is a union of
closures of conormal bundles
T^{*}_{S}M
of some strata
S\in{\mathscr{S}}
. Then the explicit characterization of the micro-suport
\mu supp(Rk_{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})

is given as follows (see, e.g.,
[
113
, Proposition 5.0.1]
).

Proposition 3.13
.

Let
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{{\mathscr{S}}-wc}(X;R)
be given. Then

\overline{T^{*}_{S}M}\subset\mu supp(Rk_{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\iff NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},S)\not\simeq 0\>.

For the applications of stratified Morse theory for constructible sheaves, it is important to get information about this
normal Morse datum

NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},S)
for a stratum
S\in{\mathscr{S}}
. In our complex context this can be obtained in the following way. Since it is a local study,
we can assume that for a given point
x\in S
we have (locally) a closed embedding
(X,x)\hookrightarrow({\mathbb{C}}^{n},x=0)
.

We already know that the isomorphism class of the “normal Morse
datum”
NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},S)
only depends on the non-degenerate covector
\omega=d\hat{f}_{x}\in T^{*}_{S}{\mathbb{C}}^{n}|_{x}
. So one can use
a
holomorphic
function germ
g\colon({\mathbb{C}}^{n},x)\to({\mathbb{C}},0)
with
d\hat{f}_{x}=d(Re(g))_{x}
. Also choose a local
normal slice

N
at
x\in S\hookrightarrow X\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{n}
, with
\{x\}=S\cap N
a point stratum of the induced Whitney stratification
{\mathscr{S}}|_{N}
of
X\cap N
.
Then one gets (see Remark
4.18
and also, e.g.,
[
113
, Proposition 5.0.3]
).

Proposition 3.14
.

Let
k_{x}\colon\{x\}\to N\cap X
be the inclusion of this point stratum, and
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{{\mathscr{S}}-wc}(X;R)
be given. Then there exists two
distinguished triangles

(27)

\begin{CD}R\Gamma(l_{X},{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})[-1]@>{}>{}>NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},S)@>{}>{}>k_{x}^{*}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}|_{N\cap X})@>{}>{}>\>,\end{CD}

and

(28)

\begin{CD}k_{x}^{!}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}|_{N\cap X})@>{}>{}>NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},S)@>{}>{}>R\Gamma(l_{X},\partial l_{X},{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})[-1]@>{}>{}>\>.\end{CD}

Moreover, the isomorphism classes of
R\Gamma(l_{X},{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})
and

R\Gamma(l_{X},\partial l_{X},{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})
only depend
on the stratum
S
of
X
(but on no other choice like the local embedding
(X,x)\hookrightarrow({\mathbb{C}}^{n},x=0)
, or the choice of
N
or
g
as above).

Here we use the following notation (with
0<|w|\ll\delta\ll 1
and
r(z):=\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}z_{i}\bar{z}_{i}
the square of the local distance to
x=0\in{\mathbb{C}}^{n}
)

(29)

(l_{X},\partial l_{X}):=(X\cap N\cap\{r\leq\delta,g=w\},X\cap N\cap\{r=\delta,g=w\})

for the
complex link
of
X
with respect to
S\in{\mathscr{S}}

(see
[
47
, p.161, Definition.2.2]
). In other words, this is the local
Milnor fiber (with its boundary)
(see, e.g.,
[
74
]
or
[
113
, Example 1.1.2]
) of the holomorphic function germ

g\colon(X\cap N,x)\to({\mathbb{C}},0)
, which has an isolated stratified critical point in
x
with respect to
{\mathscr{S}}|_{N}
(since
dg_{x}
is a non-degenerate covector).

Example 3.15
.

Assume
S\in{\mathscr{S}}
is an
open
stratum in
X
. Then for
x\in S
and a normal slice
N
at
x
one gets
X\cap N=\{x\}
, so that the corresponding complex
link is empty,
l_{X}=\emptyset
, with

NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},S)\simeq k_{x}^{*}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}|_{N\cap X})\simeq i_{x}^{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}

just the stalk of
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{{\mathscr{S}}-wc}(X;R)
at
x
.

Example 3.16
.

We state here cohomological counterparts of some results of
[
47
]
.

(1)

If we take
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}={\mathbb{Z}}_{X}
, then we get by the first distinguished
triangle (
27
) for the cohomology of the “normal Morse datum”
NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},S)
for
x\in S
:

\bigl(R^{k}\Gamma_{\{Re(g)|_{N\cap X}\geq 0\}}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}|_{N\cap X})\bigr)_{x}\simeq\begin{cases}{\mathbb{Z}}&\text{for $k=0$ and $l_{X}=\emptyset$,}\\
0&\text{for $k\neq 0$ and $l_{X}=\emptyset$,}\\
\widetilde{H}^{k-1}(l_{X};{\mathbb{Z}})&\text{for $l_{X}\neq\emptyset$.}\end{cases}

This corresponds to the “first part of the fundamental theorem of complex stratified
Morse theory”
[
47
, p.16, Theorem CSMT Part A, p.166 Corollary 2.4.1]
.

(2)

Consider now the case
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}=Rj_{*}{\mathbb{Z}}_{U}
for
j\colon U\hookrightarrow X

the inclusion of an open subspace, which is a union of strata. Assume

x\not\in U
.
Then one gets by the second distinguished
triangle (
28
) for the cohomology of the “normal Morse datum”
NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},S)
for
x\in S
:

\bigl(R^{k}\Gamma_{\{Re(g)|_{N\cap X}\geq 0\}}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}|_{N\cap X})\bigr)_{x}\simeq H^{k-1}(l_{X},\partial l_{X};Rj_{*}{\mathbb{Z}}_{U})\>.

Moreover, by the base change isomorphism (
11
) one gets

H^{k-1}(l_{X},\partial l_{X};Rj_{*}{\mathbb{Z}}_{U})\simeq H^{k-1}(l_{U},\partial l_{U};{\mathbb{Z}})\>,

with
(l_{U},\partial l_{U}):=(l_{X},\partial l_{X})\cap U

the
complex link of
U
.
This corresponds to the “second part of the fundamental theorem of complex stratified
Morse theory”
[
47
, p.18, Theorem CSMT Part B, p.169, Corollary 2.6.1]
.

Proposition
3.14
and the corresponding distinguished triangles for
dg_{x}\in T^{*}_{S}{\mathbb{C}}^{n}
a non-degenerate covector are related to the theory
of the
nearby and vanishing cycle functors
of Deligne, as will be explained and discussed in later sections (see Remark
4.18
):

(30)

NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},S)\simeq\left(\,\varphi_{g|_{N\cap X}}[-1]({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}|_{N\cap X})\,\right)_{x}

and

(31)

R\Gamma(l_{X},{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\simeq\left(\,\psi_{g|_{N\cap X}}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}|_{N\cap X})\,\right)_{x}\>,

resp.,

(32)

R\Gamma(l_{X},\partial l_{X},{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\simeq k_{x}^{!}\left(\,\psi_{g|_{N\cap X}}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}|_{N\cap X})\,\right)\>.

One also has for a holomorphic function germ
g\colon(X,x)\to({\mathbb{C}},0)
and

{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{{\mathscr{S}}-wc}(X;R)
an isomorphism (see Corollary
4.14
or, e.g.,
[
113
, Corollary 1.1.1]
)

(33)

LMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},Re(g),x)=\bigl(\,R\Gamma_{\{Re(g)\geq 0\}}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\,\bigr)_{x}\simeq\left(\,\varphi_{g}[-1]({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\,\right)_{x}\>.

Example 3.17

(Normal Morse data for products)
.

Let
X_{i}
be a complex algebraic (or analytic) variety, with

{\mathscr{F}}_{i}^{\bullet}
weakly constructible
with respect to a Whitney stratification
{\mathscr{S}}_{i}
of
X_{i}
, for
i=1,2
. Then
{\mathscr{F}}_{1}^{\bullet}\overset{L}{\boxtimes}{\mathscr{F}}_{2}^{\bullet}
is
weakly constructible with respect to the product Whitney stratification
{\mathscr{S}}_{1}\times{\mathscr{S}}_{2}
of
X_{1}\times X_{2}
(with strata
S_{1}\times S_{2}
for

S_{1}\in{\mathscr{S}}_{1}
and
S_{2}\in{\mathscr{S}}_{2}
). Moreover

NMD\left({\mathscr{F}}_{1}^{\bullet}\overset{L}{\boxtimes}{\mathscr{F}}_{2}^{\bullet},S_{1}\times S_{2}\right)\simeq NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}_{1},S_{1})\overset{L}{\otimes}NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}_{2},S_{2})\>.

Let
N_{i}\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{n_{i}}
be normal slices at
x_{i}\in S_{i}
, with a corresponding non-degenerate covector
dg_{i,x_{i}}\in T^{*}_{S_{i}}{\mathbb{C}}^{n_{i}}

for a holomorphic function germ
g_{i}\colon(\mathbb{C}^{n_{i}},x_{i})\to({\mathbb{C}},0)
as above (
i=1,2
). Then
N=N_{1}\times N_{2}\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{n_{1}}\times{\mathbb{C}}^{n_{2}}
is a normal slice at
(x_{1},x_{2})\in S_{1}\times S_{2}
, with
dg_{(x_{1},x_{2})}=\left(dg_{1,x_{1}},dg_{2,x_{2}}\right)
a corresponding non-degenerate covector
defined by
g(z_{1},z_{2}):=g_{1}(z_{1})+g_{2}(z_{2})
. Example
3.17
is then by (
30
) a very special case of the

Thom-Sebastiani Theorem for vanishing cycles
stated in Theorem
4.30
.

3.2.
Perverse sheaf description via normal Morse data

The distinguished triangles of Proposition
3.14
can be used to get
(inductive) information about the “normal Morse data”.
The advantage in the complex context comes from the fact
that
X\cap N\cap\{g=w\}
is a complex analytic space (of lower dimension) with an induced
Whitney stratification
.
Moreover, the distance function
r
used for the
stratified Morse theory on
X\cap N\cap\{g=w\}
(in the induction step)
is now
strongly plurisubharmonic
so that one has nice estimates
for the Morse index (of an approximation by a Morse function) of
r

and
-r
.

Definition 3.18
.

A
C^{k}
-function
f\colon X\to{\mathbb{R}}
(
k\geq 2
)
is called
q-convex in

x\in X
,
if one can choose
\hat{f}\colon({\mathbb{C}}^{n},x)\to({\mathbb{R}},f(x))
in a local embedding
(X,x)\hookrightarrow({\mathbb{C}}^{n},x)
with
\hat{f}|_{X}=f

so that its
Levi form

L_{\hat{f}}(x)
at the point
x
has at most
q

non-positive eigenvalues. Here the Levi form of
\hat{f}
at the point
x

is the Hermitian form given by the matrix of partial derivatives:

L_{\hat{f}}(x):=\left(\;\frac{\partial^{2}\hat{f}}{\partial z_{j}\,\partial\bar{z}_{k}}(x)\;\right)_{1\leq j,k\leq n}\>.

f
is called
q-convex
(in
a subset
Z\subset X
) if it is q-convex in
all points (of
Z
).

For example, a
strongly plurisubharmonic

C^{k}
-function on an open subset
U
of
{\mathbb{C}}^{n}
(with
k\geq 2
) is

0
-convex in all of
U
. In particular, the distance function

r\colon{\mathbb{C}}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{\geq 0}\,;\>r(z):=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\>z_{i}\bar{z}_{i}\quad\text{is $0$-convex in ${\mathbb{C}}^{n}$.}

We next state the second
main result
for stratified Morse theory of constructible sheaves in the complex context
(see
[
113
, Theorem 6.0.1]
for a more general formulation). We include here a complete proof based on Theorem
3.12
,
for explaining the use of stratified Morse theory and because similar ideas can also be used later on for other important results.

Theorem 3.19
.

Let
{\mathscr{S}}
be a Whitney
stratification of the complex algebraic (or analytic) variety
X
, and consider a

C^{\infty}
-function
f\colon X\to{\mathbb{R}}
.
Assume
a<b
are stratified regular values of
f
with respect to
{\mathscr{S}}

such that
\{a\leq f\leq b\}
is compact. Let
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{{\mathscr{S}}-(w)c}(X;R)
be given.

(1)

Suppose
f
is q-convex in
\{a\leq f\leq b\}
, and let
q^{\prime}:=\min\{q,\dim X\}
.

{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in\;^{p}D^{\leq n}_{{\mathscr{S}}}(X;R)\>\Rightarrow\>R\Gamma(\{a\leq f\leq b\},\{f=a\},{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\in\;^{p}D^{\leq n+q^{\prime}}(\{pt\};R)\>.

If
R
is a Dedekind domain and
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}
is
{\mathscr{S}}
-constructible, then also

{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in\;^{p^{+}}D^{\leq n}_{{\mathscr{S}}}(X;R)\>\Rightarrow\>R\Gamma(\{a\leq f\leq b\},\{f=a\},{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\in\;^{p^{+}}D^{\leq n+q^{\prime}}(\{pt\};R)\>.

(2)

Suppose
-f
is q-convex in
\{a\leq f\leq b\}
, and let
q^{\prime}:=\min\{q,\dim X\}
.

{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in\;^{p}D^{\geq n}_{{\mathscr{S}}}(X;R)\>\Rightarrow\>R\Gamma(\{a\leq f\leq b\},\{f=a\},{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\in\;^{p}D^{\geq n-q^{\prime}}(\{pt\};R)\>.

If
R
is a Dedekind domain and
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}
is
{\mathscr{S}}
-constructible, then also

{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in\;^{p^{+}}D^{\geq n}_{{\mathscr{S}}}(X;R)\>\Rightarrow\>R\Gamma(\{a\leq f\leq b\},\{f=a\},{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\in\;^{p^{+}}D^{\geq n-q^{\prime}}(\{pt\};R)\>.

Here
R\Gamma(\{a\leq f\leq b\},\{f=a\},{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\in D^{b}_{c}(\{pt\};R)
in case
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}
is
{\mathscr{S}}
-constructible.

Proof.

We prove the claim by induction over
\dim X
, where the case

\dim X=0
is trivial (since the conditions to be checked contain the zero object and are stable by finite direct sums).

After approximating
f
by a stratified Morse function (see
[
8
]
), we can assume that
f
has only finitely many stratified Morse critical points
in
\{a\leq f\leq b\}
.
If the approximation is close enough, then also this new
\pm f
is q-convex.
This implies the following important estimate for the
Morse index
\tau(f|_{S})
of
f|_{S}
for a stratum
S\in{\mathscr{S}}
in terms of
q^{\prime}:=\min\{q,\dim(X)\}

(compare with
[
47
, p.191, Lemma 4.A.2]
):

(34)

\begin{cases}\tau(f|_{S})\leq\dim S+q^{\prime},&\quad\text{if $f$ is q-convex.}\\
\tau(f|_{S})=2\dim S-\tau(-f|_{S})\geq\dim S-q^{\prime},&\quad\text{if $-f$ is q-convex.}\end{cases}

Then we apply Lemma
3.8
.
Since the conditions to be checked contain the zero object and are stable by extensions,
we only have to show that the
“local Morse datum”

LMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},f,x)=(R\Gamma_{\{f\geq f(x)\}}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}))_{x}

of
f
at a stratified Morse
critical point
x
in a stratum
S\in{\mathscr{S}}
belongs to
\;{}^{p^{(+)}}D^{\leq n+q^{\prime}}(\{pt\};R)
for statement 1.
(resp., belongs to
\;{}^{p^{(+)}}D^{\geq n+q^{\prime}}(\{pt\};R)
for statement 2.).

By Theorem
3.12
we get

LMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},f,x)\simeq NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},S)[-\tau],

with
NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},S)
the “normal Morse datum” of
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}
in the stratum
S

and
\tau
the Morse index of
f|_{S}
.
Here we can use the
estimate (
34
) for this Morse index.
Since the conditions to be checked contain the zero object and are stable by extensions as well as shifting by
[1]
(resp.,
[-1]
) for the first (resp., second) result, we only have to show that (with
s:=\dim S
):

(35)

NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},S)\in\begin{cases}\;{}^{p^{(+)}}D^{\leq n-s}(\{pt\};R)\quad&\text{in part 1. of the theorem.}\\
\;{}^{p^{(+)}}D^{\geq n-s}(\{pt\};R)\quad&\text{in part 2. of the theorem.}\end{cases}

By Proposition
3.14
we have two distinguished triangles:

\begin{CD}R\Gamma(l_{X},{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})[-1]@>{}>{}>NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},S)@>{}>{}>k_{x}^{*}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}|_{N\cap X})@>{}>{}>\>,\end{CD}

and

\begin{CD}k_{x}^{!}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}|_{N\cap X})@>{}>{}>NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},S)@>{}>{}>R\Gamma(l_{X},\partial l_{X},{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})[-1]@>{}>{}>\>,\end{CD}

where we use, as before, the notation (with
0<|w|\ll\delta\ll 1
)

(l_{X},\partial l_{X}):=(X\cap N\cap\{r\leq\delta,g=w\},X\cap N\cap\{r=\delta,g=w\})

for the
complex link
of
X
with respect to
S\in{\mathscr{S}}
.
Also,
k_{x}\colon\{x\}\hookrightarrow N\cap X

is the inclusion, and
r
is the
0-convex distance function

r(z):=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\>z_{i}\bar{z}_{i}
in our fixed local embedding
(X,x)\hookrightarrow({\mathbb{C}}^{n},x=0)
.

But
N\cap\{g=w\}
is
transversal
to
{\mathscr{S}}
near
x
(for

0<|w|\ll 1
). So

L:=X\cap N\cap\{g=w\}

gets an induced Whitney stratification
{\mathscr{S}}|_{L}
. Moreover,
for
0<|w|\ll\delta\ll 1
,
\delta
is a regular value of
r
with respect to

{\mathscr{S}}|_{L}
, since
dg_{x}
is non-degenerate.
Then we can use the induction hypothesis with
q=0
for

\begin{cases}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}|_{L},\>r\quad&\text{and $[a,b]:=[-1,\delta]$ in part 1. of the theorem.}\\
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}|_{L},\>-r\quad&\text{and $[a,b]:=[-\delta,1]$ in part 2. of the theorem.}\end{cases}

Note that

{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}|_{L}[-1]\in\;^{p^{(+)}}D^{\leq n-s}_{{\mathscr{S}}|_{L}}(L;R)\quad\text{or}\quad\;^{p^{(+)}}D^{\geq n-s}_{{\mathscr{S}}|_{L}}(L;R)

by transversality and Proposition
2.27
(or its proof), with
\hbox{\rm codim}\,L=s+1
.

By the induction hypothesis we get

R\Gamma(l_{X},{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})[-1]\in\;^{p^{(+)}}D^{\leq n-s}(\{pt\};R)

or

R\Gamma(l_{X},\partial l_{X},{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})[-1]\in\;^{p^{(+)}}D^{\geq n-s}(\{pt\};R)\>.

But, by definition,
k_{x}^{*}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}|_{N\cap X})\in\;^{p^{(+)}}D^{\leq n-s}(\{pt\};R)
,
which implies part 1. of the theorem by the first distinguished triangle above.
Similarly

{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}|_{X\cap N}\in\;^{p^{(+)}}D^{\geq n-s}_{{\mathscr{S}}|_{N}}(X\cap N;R)

by Proposition
2.27
(or its proof), so that
k_{x}^{!}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}|_{N\cap X})\in\;^{p^{(+)}}D^{\geq n-s}(\{pt\};R)
. This implies part 2. of the theorem by the second distinguished triangle above.
The last claim of Theorem
3.19
follows by inspection of the proof.
∎

Corollary 3.20
.

Let
\bar{X}
be a compact complex algebraic (or analytic) variety of dimension
d
,
with
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{wc}(\bar{X};R)
(resp.,
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{c}(\bar{X};R)
and
R
is a Dedekind domain
in case we consider the dual perverse t-structure). Let
j\colon X\hookrightarrow\bar{X}
be the inclusion of the open dense complement of a closed algebraic (or analytic) subset
Z\subset\bar{X}
(so that
d=\dim X=\dim\bar{X}
). Then:

(1)

j^{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in\;^{p^{(+)}}D^{\leq n}(X;R)\>\Rightarrow\>Rj_{!}j^{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in\;^{p^{(+)}}D^{\leq n}(\bar{X};R)\>

\Rightarrow\>R\Gamma_{c}(X,j^{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\simeq R\Gamma(\bar{X},Rj_{!}j^{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\in\;^{p^{(+)}}D^{\leq n+d}(\{pt\};R)\>.

(2)

j^{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in\;^{p^{(+)}}D^{\geq n}(X;R)\>\Rightarrow\>Rj_{*}j^{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in\;^{p^{(+)}}D^{\geq n}(\bar{X};R)\>

\Rightarrow\>R\Gamma(X,j^{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\simeq R\Gamma(\bar{X},Rj_{*}j^{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\in\;^{p^{(+)}}D^{\geq n-d}(\{pt\};R)\>.

Note that by a theorem of Nagata (see, e.g.,
[
82
,
23
]
) such a compactification is always available in the complex algebraic context.

The (method of) proof of Theorem
3.19
also implies the following.

Proposition 3.21
.

Let
{\mathscr{S}}
be a Whitney
stratification of the complex algebraic (or analytic) variety
X
, and consider a
differentiable function
f\colon X\to{\mathbb{R}}
.
Assume
a<b
are stratified regular values of
f
with respect to
{\mathscr{S}}

such that
\{a\leq f\leq b\}
is compact.
Let
T\subset D^{b}(\{pt\};R)
be a fixed “null system”, i.e., a full triangulated subcategory stable by isomorphisms.

(1)

Denote by
D^{b}_{({\mathscr{S}}-)T-stalk}(X;R)
the induced “null system” given by all complexes
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{({\mathscr{S}}-)wc}(X;R)
with stalks

i_{x}^{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in T
for all
x\in X
. Then

{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{{\mathscr{S}}-T-stalk}(X;R)\>\Rightarrow\>R\Gamma(\{a\leq f\leq b\},\{f=a\},{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\in T\>.

(2)

Denote by
D^{b}_{({\mathscr{S}}-)T-costalk}(X;R)
the induced “null system” given by all
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{({\mathscr{S}}-)wc}(X;R)
with costalks

i_{x}^{!}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in T
for all
x\in X
. Then

{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{{\mathscr{S}}-T-costalk}(X;R)\>\Rightarrow\>R\Gamma(\{a\leq f\leq b\},\{f=a\},{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\in T\>.

Corollary 3.22
.

Let
\bar{X}
be a compact complex algebraic (or analytic) variety,
with
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{wc}(\bar{X};R)
. Let
j\colon X\hookrightarrow\bar{X}
be the inclusion of the open complement of a closed algebraic (or analytic)
subset
Z\subset\bar{X}
, and fix a given “null system”
T\subset D^{b}(\{pt\};R)
.

(1)

j^{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{T-stalk}(X;R)\>\Rightarrow\>Rj_{!}j^{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{T-stalk}(\bar{X};R)\>

\Rightarrow\>R\Gamma_{c}(X,j^{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\simeq R\Gamma(\bar{X},Rj_{!}j^{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\in T\>.

(2)

j^{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{T-costalk}(X;R)\>\Rightarrow\>Rj_{*}j^{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{T-costalk}(\bar{X};R)\>

\Rightarrow\>R\Gamma(X,j^{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\simeq R\Gamma(\bar{X},Rj_{*}j^{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\in T\>.

Example 3.23
.

Let us mention here some important examples of such a “null system”
T\subset D^{b}(\{pt\};R)
.

(1)

T=D^{b}_{c}(\{pt\};R)
are the complexes with finitely generated cohomology.
Then
D^{b}_{({\mathscr{S}}-)T-stalk}(X;R)
is the category of (
{\mathscr{S}}
-)constructible sheaf complexes.

(2)

Assume
R
is a Dedekind domain and
T=D^{b}_{tc}(\{pt\};R)
are the complexes with finitely generated torsion cohomology.
Then
D^{b}_{({\mathscr{S}}-)T-stalk}(X;R)
is the category of (
{\mathscr{S}}
-)constructible torsion sheaf complexes.

(3)

Assume
R
is a field and
T=D^{b}_{c,\chi=0}(\{pt\};R)
are the complexes with finitely generated cohomology whose Euler characteristic is zero.
Then
D^{b}_{({\mathscr{S}}-)T-stalk}(X;R)
is the category of (
{\mathscr{S}}
-)constructible sheaf complexes
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{({\mathscr{S}}-)c}(X;R)

with associated (
{\mathscr{S}}
-)constructible function

\chi_{stalk}\left({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\right)=0\in CF_{({\mathscr{S}})}(X)\>,

i.e., whose stalkwise Euler characteristic
\chi_{stalk}\left({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\right)(x):=\chi\left({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}_{x}\right)
vanishes for all
x\in X
.
Then
[{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}]\in K_{0}\left(D^{b}_{({\mathscr{S}}-)c}(X;R)\right)
is in the kernel of

\chi_{stalk}\colon\>K_{0}\left(D^{b}_{({\mathscr{S}})-c}(X;R)\right)\to CF_{({\mathscr{S}})}(X)\>\iff{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{({\mathscr{S}}-)T-stalk}(X;R)\>.

Example 3.24
.

Let
\bar{X}
be a compact complex algebraic (or analytic) variety,
with a given Whitney stratification
{\mathscr{S}}
. Let
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},{\mathscr{G}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{{\mathscr{S}}-c}(\bar{X};R)
and assume
R
a field. Let
j\colon X\hookrightarrow\bar{X}
be the inclusion of the open complement of a closed algebraic (or analytic)
subset
Z\subset\bar{X}
, which is a union of strata
S\in{\mathscr{S}}
, with
{\mathscr{S}}|_{X}
the induced Whitney stratification of
X
.
Then
\chi_{stalk}(j^{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})=\chi_{stalk}(j^{*}{\mathscr{G}}^{\bullet})
as a constructible function implies

\chi_{c}(X,j^{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})=\chi_{c}(X,j^{*}{\mathscr{G}}^{\bullet})\>.

So the global Euler characteristic with compact support
\chi_{c}(X,j^{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})
only depends on the underlying constructible function

\chi_{stalk}(j^{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})=\alpha\in CF_{{\mathscr{S}}}(X)
, with

(36)

\chi_{c}(X,{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})=\int_{X}\alpha d\chi:=\sum_{S\in{\mathscr{S}}|_{X}}\>\chi_{c}(S)\cdot\alpha(S)\>.

Here
\chi_{c}(S):=\chi_{c}(S,R_{S})=\chi(H_{c}^{*}(S;R))
is the corresponding
Euler characteristic of a stratum
S\in{\mathscr{S}}|_{X}
.

Similar considerations apply if
X
is given as a relatively compact open subset
X=\{f<b\}
(resp., a compact subset
X=\{f\leq b\}
) for a proper differentiable function
f\colon\bar{X}\to[a,d[\;\subset{\mathbb{R}}
,
with
b<d
a stratified regular value of
f
with respect to
{\mathscr{S}}
. Here
\bar{X}
does not need to be compact, with strata
S
of
{\mathscr{S}}|_{\{f<b\}}
given by the connected components of
S^{\prime}\cap\{f<b\}
for
S^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{S}}
. Similarly for the decomposition
{\mathscr{S}}|_{\{f\leq b\}}
of
\{f\leq b\}
, but here the parts

S^{\prime}\cap\{f\leq b\}
for
S^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{S}}
are complex manifolds with possible non-empty boundary
S^{\prime}\cap\{f=b\}
.

The proof of Theorem
3.19
implies directly the following characterizations (see, e.g.,
[
113
, Corollary 6.0.2]
).

Corollary 3.25
.

Let
{\mathscr{S}}
be a Whitney
stratification of the complex algebraic (or analytic) variety
X
, and
consider
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{{\mathscr{S}}-wc}(X;R)
(resp.,
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{c}(X;R)
and
R
is a Dedekind domain
in case we consider the dual perverse t-structure).
Then we have:

(1)

{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in\,^{p^{(+)}}D^{\leq 0}_{{\mathscr{S}}}(X;R)

\iff

NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},S)[-\dim(S)]\in\,^{p^{(+)}}D^{\leq 0}(\{pt\};R)

for all strata
S\in{\mathscr{S}}
.

(2)

{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in\,^{p^{(+)}}D^{\geq 0}_{{\mathscr{S}}}(X;R)

\iff

NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},S)[-\dim(S)]\in\,^{p^{(+)}}D^{\geq 0}(\{pt\};R)

for all strata
S\in{\mathscr{S}}
.

(3)

Let
T\subset D^{b}(\{pt\};R)
be a fixed ‘‘null system’’. Then

\displaystyle{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{{\mathscr{S}}-T-stalk}(X;R)

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},S)\in T\quad\text{for all strata $S\in{\mathscr{S}}$}

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{{\mathscr{S}}-T-costalk}(X;R)\>.

Proof.

The implications
\Rightarrow
are already contained in (
35
) from the proof of Theorem
3.19
.
For the other implications
\Leftarrow
in case of a point stratum
S=\{x\}
one applies the proof of Theorem
3.19

for the (co)stalk description

i_{x}^{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\simeq R\Gamma(\{r\leq\delta\},{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\simeq R\Gamma(\{-1\leq r\leq\delta\},\{r=-1\},{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})

and

i_{x}^{!}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\simeq R\Gamma_{c}(\{r<\delta\},{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\simeq R\Gamma(\{-\delta\leq-r\leq 1\},\{-r=-\delta\},{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\>,

with
0<\delta
small and
r
the
0-convex distance function

r(z):=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\>z_{i}\bar{z}_{i}
in some fixed local embedding
(X,x)\hookrightarrow({\mathbb{C}}^{n},x=0)
.
The general case is reduced to a point stratum via intersecting with a normal slice
N
to
S\in{\mathscr{S}}
at a given point
x\in S
.
Here, the normal Morse data
NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},S^{\prime})=NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}|_{N},S^{\prime}\cap N)
for
S^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{S}}
close to
x
does not change.
∎

Example 3.26
.

Let
{\mathscr{S}}
be a Whitney
stratification of the complex algebraic (or analytic) variety
X
, with
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{{\mathscr{S}}-wc}(X;R)
. Then
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}
is a
perverse sheaf
if and only if

H^{i}\left(NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},S)[-\dim(S)]\right)=0\quad\text{for $i\neq 0$ and all strata $S\in{\mathscr{S}}$.}

If, moreover,
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}
is constructible and
R
a Dedekind domain, then
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}
is a
strongly perverse sheaf
if and only if, in addition,

H^{0}\left(NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},S)[-\dim(S)]\right)\quad\text{ is finitely generated and torsion-free }

for all strata
S\in{\mathscr{S}}
.

Example 3.27
.

Let
{\mathscr{S}}
be a Whitney
stratification of the complex algebraic (or analytic) variety
X
, with
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},{\mathscr{G}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{{\mathscr{S}}-c}(X;R)
and
R
a field.
Then
\chi_{stalk}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})=\chi_{stalk}({\mathscr{G}}^{\bullet})
as an
{\mathscr{S}}
-constructible function if and only if

\chi\left(NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},S)\right)=\chi\left(NMD({\mathscr{G}}^{\bullet},S)\right)\quad\text{for all strata $S\in{\mathscr{S}}$.}

So the Euler characteristic
\chi\left(NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},S)\right)=:\chi\left(NMD(\alpha,S)\right)
for
S\in{\mathscr{S}}
depends only on the underlying
{\mathscr{S}}
-constructible function

\alpha=\chi_{stalk}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})
.

Remark 3.28
.

Corollary
3.25
also shows that the corresponding properties of the normal Morse data do not depend on the choice of the
Whitney stratification
{\mathscr{S}}
of
X
with
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}

{\mathscr{S}}
-(weakly) constructible.
The given characterization of the perverse t-structure in terms of normal Morse data is equivalent (in an embedded context) to

[
67
, Theorem 10.3.12]
, if one identifies the normal Morse data with the corresponding “micro-local type”
in the sense of Kashiwara-Schapira
[
67
, Proposition 6.6.1(ii)]

(as shown in
[
113
, Equation (5.52) on p.311]
).
The characterization of perverse sheaves in terms of normal Morse data corresponds to
the “dimension axiom” in the Morse theoretic approach to perverse sheaves
as outlined in
[
84
]
.
It also implies the
purity result
stated in
[
47
, p.223, 6.A.3]
.

Here are the final applications of Theorem
3.19
for this section (see, e.g.,
[
113
, Corollary 6.0.5]
),
which for
Y
a point space reduce to Corollary
3.20
.

Proposition 3.29
.

Let
\bar{f}\colon\bar{X}\to Y
be a proper holomorphic map of complex varieties,
with
Z\subset\bar{X}
a closed complex subvariety. Consider the induced holomorphic
map

f:=\bar{f}|_{X}\colon\,X:=\bar{X}\backslash Z\to Y\>.

Suppose the fiber dimension of
f
is bounded above by
d
, and consider

{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{(w)c}(X;R)
(resp.,
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{c}(X;R)
and
R
is a Dedekind domain
in case we consider the dual perverse t-structure). Then:

(1)

{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}|_{X}\in\,^{p^{(+)}}D^{\geq n}(X;R)\>\Rightarrow\>Rf_{*}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}|_{X})\in\,^{p^{(+)}}D^{\geq n-d}(Y;R)
.

(2)

{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}|_{X}\in\,^{p^{(+)}}D^{\leq n}(X;R)\>\Rightarrow\>Rf_{!}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}|_{X})\in\,^{p^{(+)}}D^{\leq n+d}(Y;R)
.

Note that by a theorem of Nagata (see, e.g.,
[
82
,
23
]
) such a (partial) compactification
\bar{f}\colon\bar{X}\to Y

is always available in the complex algebraic context for a morphism
f\colon X\to Y
.

Corollary 3.30
.

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a morphism of complex algebraic varieties.
Suppose the fiber dimension of
f
is bounded above by
d
, and consider

{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{(w)c}(X;R)
(resp.,
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{c}(X;R)
and
R
is a Dedekind domain
in case we consider the dual perverse t-structure). Then:

(1)

{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in\,^{p^{(+)}}D^{\geq n}(X;R)\>\Rightarrow\>Rf_{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in\,^{p^{(+)}}D^{\geq n-d}(Y;R)
.

(2)

{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in\,^{p^{(+)}}D^{\leq n}(X;R)\>\Rightarrow\>Rf_{!}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in\,^{p^{(+)}}D^{\leq n+d}(Y;R)
.

Example 3.31

(Finite morphism)
.

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a
finite
morphism of complex algebraic (or analytic) varieties (i.e.,
f
is proper with fiber dimension
d=0
).
Then
Rf_{!}=Rf_{*}=f_{*}
is
t-exact
with respect to the perverse t-structure, and in case
R
a Dedekind domain also with respect to the dual
t-structure.

3.3.
Characteristic cycles and index theorems

In this section, we give an introduction to the theory
of Lagrangian cycles in the complex analytic and algebraic context,
using the language of
stratified
Morse theory for constructible functions and sheaves
, as developed in the previous sections.
We explain from this viewpoint the Euler isomorphism between
constructible functions and Lagrangian cycles, together with some index theorems.

The theory of Lagrangian cycles in the complex analytic context started in
1973

with Kashiwara’s local index formula for holonomic D-modules, as formulated in

[
62
]
and proved in
[
64
, Chapter 6]
(compare also with
[
85
]
).
Kashiwara introduced for this a local invariant of a singular complex analytic set,
which around the same time was independently introduced by MacPherson
as the
local Euler obstruction
in his celebrated work
[
83
]
on
Chern homology
classes for singular complex algebraic varieties (establishing a conjecture
of Grothendieck and Deligne).
It was Dubson
[
34
,
35
,
15
]
who observed some years later that these two invariants are the same. The definitions
of Kashiwara and MacPherson are of transcendental nature. A purely algebraic
definition of the ”local Euler obstruction” was found by Gonzalez-Sprinberg and
Verdier
[
22
, Exposé 1]
(compare with
[
41
, Example 4.2.9]
).

In this section we work in a global embedded context, with
k\colon X\hookrightarrow M
the closed embedding of a complex algebraic (or analytic) variety
X
into a complex algebraic (or analytic) manifold
M
of dimension
\dim M=m
(with
M
pure-dimensional or
\dim M
viewed as a locally constant function).
Let
{\mathscr{S}}
be a given Whitney stratification of
X
, with conormal space

T^{*}_{{\mathscr{S}}}M:=\bigcup_{S\in{\mathscr{S}}}\>T^{*}_{S}M\hookrightarrow T^{*}M|_{X}\hookrightarrow T^{*}M\>.

Here
T^{*}_{{\mathscr{S}}}M
is a closed subset by the
Whitney a-condition
of our stratification
{\mathscr{S}}
.
The open subset of
non-degenerate
covectors is given by

\left(T^{*}_{{\mathscr{S}}}M\right)^{\circ}=\bigcup_{S\in{\mathscr{S}}}\>\left(T^{*}_{S}M\right)^{\circ}\>,

with

\left(T^{*}_{S}M\right)^{\circ}:=T^{*}_{S}M\backslash\bigcup_{S\neq S^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{S}}}\overline{T^{*}_{S^{\prime}}M}\>.

Definition 3.32
.

The abelian group
L({\mathscr{S}},T^{*}M)
of (
{\mathbb{C}}^{*}
-conic)
Lagrangian cycles
in
T^{*}_{{\mathscr{S}}}M
is given by

L({\mathscr{S}},T^{*}M):=H^{BM}_{2m}\left(T^{*}_{{\mathscr{S}}}M;{\mathbb{Z}}\right)\>,

so that a corresponding Lagrangian cycle is uniquely given by a (locally) finite sum

(37)

\sum_{S\in{\mathscr{S}}}m(S)\cdot\left[\overline{T^{*}_{S}M}\right]\quad\text{with $m(S)\in{\mathbb{Z}}$,}

and
\left[\overline{T^{*}_{S}M}\right]
the corresponding
fundamental class
.
If we do not want to fix the stratification
{\mathscr{S}}
, then we denote by
L(T^{*}M|_{X})
the abelian group of (
{\mathbb{C}}^{*}
-conic)
Lagrangian cycles
in

T^{*}M|_{X}
given by a similar (locally) finite
{\mathbb{Z}}
-linear combination of fundamental classes

\left[T^{*}_{Z}M\right]:=\left[\overline{T^{*}_{Z_{reg}}M}\right]

with
Z\hookrightarrow X
an irreducible closed algebraic (or analytic) subvariety.

Remark 3.33
.

In the complex algebraic context, the Borel-Moore homology group

H^{BM}_{2m}\left(T^{*}_{{\mathscr{S}}}M;{\mathbb{Z}}\right)\simeq A_{m}\left(T^{*}_{{\mathscr{S}}}M\right)\simeq Z_{m}\left(T^{*}_{{\mathscr{S}}}M\right)

is also the same as the corresponding
Chow- and cycle group
of
T^{*}_{{\mathscr{S}}}M
as in
[
41
]
, since
T^{*}_{{\mathscr{S}}}M
is of dimension

m
.
So many of the following results could also be stated in this language, but this does not apply to our method of proof based on
stratified Morse
theory for constructible functions and sheaves
. For this reason, we work in this section only with the homological language, which at the same time
also applies to the complex analytic context.

For simplicity, in this section we only consider (
{\mathscr{S}}
-)constructible sheaf complexes in
D^{b}_{({\mathscr{S}}-)c}(-;R)
with
R
a field and their corresponding (compactly supported)

Euler characteristic
, even though most of our results and proofs work for more general “stalk properties and corresponding additive functions”
(see, e.g.,
[
113
, Section 5.0.3]
).

Definition 3.34
.

The
characteristic cycle

CC({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\in L({\mathscr{S}},T^{*}M)
of a constructible complex

{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{{\mathscr{S}}-c}(X;R)
is defined via the following multiplicities in (
37
):

(38)

m(S):=(-1)^{\dim S}\cdot\chi(NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},S))\in{\mathbb{Z}}\quad\text{for a stratum $S\in{\mathscr{S}}$.}

By Example
3.27
the Euler characteristic

\chi(NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},S))=\chi(NMD(\alpha,S))

only depends on the associated
{\mathscr{S}}
-constructible function

\alpha=\chi_{stalk}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\in CF_{{\mathscr{S}}}(X)
so that we can also define the characteristic cycle
CC(\alpha)
of
\alpha\in CF_{{\mathscr{S}}}(X)
via

(39)

(-1)^{\dim S}\cdot m(S):=\chi(NMD(\alpha,S))=\alpha(x)-\int_{l_{X}}\alpha\,d\chi\in{\mathbb{Z}}\>,

with
x\in S
,
(l_{X},\partial l_{X})
the corresponding
complex link
of
X
in
S\in{\mathscr{S}}
and
l_{X}^{\circ}:=l_{X}\backslash\partial l_{X}
.
This can also be reformulated as

(40)

(-1)^{\dim S}\cdot m(S)=\alpha(x)-\sum_{S\subset\partial S^{\prime}}\>c(S,S^{\prime})\cdot\alpha(S^{\prime})\in{\mathbb{Z}}\>,

with
c(S,S^{\prime}):=\int_{l_{X}}1_{S^{\prime}}d\chi=\int_{l_{X}^{\circ}}1_{S^{\prime}}d\chi=\chi_{c}(l_{X}^{\circ}\cap S^{\prime},{\mathbb{Q}})
for
S\subset\partial S^{\prime}
and

S,S^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{S}}
a topological invariant of the Whitney stratification
{\mathscr{S}}
of
X
. Note that

(41)

\chi_{c}(l_{X}\cap S^{\prime},{\mathbb{Q}})=\chi_{c}(l_{X}^{\circ}\cap S^{\prime},{\mathbb{Q}})\>,

since
\partial l_{X}
is a compact real analytic Whitney stratified set with
odd-dimensional

strata so that
\chi_{c}(\partial l_{X}\cap S^{\prime},{\mathbb{Q}})=0

(see, e.g.,
[
113
, Lemma 5.0.3]
and
[
124
]
).

Here, the last equality in (
39
) follows from the distinguished triangle (
27
).
The choice of the sign
(-1)^{\dim S}
will become clear in a moment.

Example 3.35
.

Let
X\hookrightarrow M
be a
closed
complex submanifold of
M
, with strata
S\in{\mathscr{S}}
given by the connected components of
X
.
Let
{\mathscr{L}}
a local system of rank r on
X
. Then

CC({\mathscr{L}})=CC(r\cdot 1_{X})=(-1)^{\dim X}\cdot r\cdot\left[T^{*}_{X}M\right]\>.

Similarly, in the general context of the above Definition with
S\in{\mathscr{S}}
an
open
stratum
of
X
and
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{{\mathscr{S}}-c}(X;R)
.
Then by Example
3.15
,
the multiplicity
m(S)
of the characteristic cycle
CC({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})
is given by
m(S)=(-1)^{\dim S}\cdot\chi_{stalk}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})(S)\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Remark 3.36
.

Let
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{{\mathscr{S}}-c}(X;R)
be given, with
k\colon X\hookrightarrow M
the global closed embedding into the complex manifold
M
. Then for a stratum
S\in{\mathscr{S}}
we get by definition

\overline{T^{*}_{S}M}\subset supp(CC({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}))\iff\chi(NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},S))\neq 0\ .

And, in general, this is much weaker than the corresponding condition

\overline{T^{*}_{S}M}\subset\mu supp(Rk_{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\iff NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},S)\not\simeq 0

from Proposition
3.13
. So in general the inclusion

(42)

supp(CC({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}))\subset\mu supp(Rk_{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\quad\text{for ${\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{{\mathscr{S}}-c}(X;R)$}

does not need to be an equality. But Example
3.26
implies

(43)

supp(CC({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}))=\mu supp(Rk_{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\quad\text{for ${\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in Perv_{{\mathscr{S}}}(X;R)$.}

Moreover, Example
3.26
also implies that
CC({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})
is an
effective
Lagrangian cycle for
0\neq{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in Perv_{{\mathscr{S}}}(X;R)
, i.e., the multiplicity

m(S)\geq 0
for all
S\in{\mathscr{S}}
, with
m(S)>0
for
S
a top-dimensional stratum in
supp({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})
.

By induction on
\dim X
and Example
3.35
, one easily gets that the induced group homomorphism

CC\colon K_{0}(D^{b}_{{\mathscr{S}}-c}(X;R))\to L({\mathscr{S}},T^{*}M)=H^{BM}_{2m}\left(T^{*}_{{\mathscr{S}}}M;{\mathbb{Z}}\right)

is
surjective
. Moreover,
CC
factorizes over
\chi_{stalk}
and both homomorphisms

CC\colon K_{0}(D^{b}_{{\mathscr{S}}-c}(X;R))\to L({\mathscr{S}},T^{*}M)\quad\text{and}\quad CC\colon CF_{{\mathscr{S}}}(X)\to L({\mathscr{S}},T^{*}M)

have by Example
3.27
the
same kernel
, so that
CC
induces for a fixed Whitney stratification
{\mathscr{S}}
of
X
an isomorphism of abelian groups

(44)

\begin{CD}CC\colon CF_{{\mathscr{S}}}(X)@>{\sim}>{}>L({\mathscr{S}},T^{*}M)=H^{BM}_{2m}\left(T^{*}_{{\mathscr{S}}}M;{\mathbb{Z}}\right)\>.\end{CD}

Finally,
CC({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\in L(T^{*}M|_{X})
(and then also
CC(\alpha)\in L(T^{*}M|_{X})
) does not depend on the choice of the Whitney stratification
{\mathscr{S}}
with

{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{{\mathscr{S}}-c}(X;R)
. In fact, if
{\mathscr{T}}
is another Whitney stratification of
X
which refines
{\mathscr{S}}
, then any stratum
T\in{\mathscr{T}}
is contained in a stratum
S\in{\mathscr{S}}
. If
T\subset S
is
open
, then
NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},S)=NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},T)
just by the definitions. If
T\subset S
is a proper closed subset, then

NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},T)=0
. In fact, if
x\in T
is a stratified Morse critical point of a function germ
f\colon(M,x)\to({\mathbb{R}},f(x))
with respect to
{\mathscr{T}}
, then

LMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},f,x)\simeq NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},T)[-\tau]
by Theorem
3.12
. But then
x\in S
is not a stratified critical point of
f\colon(M,x)\to({\mathbb{R}},f(x))
with respect to
{\mathscr{S}}
so that
LMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},f,x)=0
by Lemma
3.3
. So in the limit over all such Whiney stratifications
{\mathscr{S}}
of
X
, we get a surjective group homomorphism

CC\colon K_{0}(D^{b}_{c}(X;R))\to L(T^{*}M|_{X})\>,

which factorizes via
\chi_{stalk}
over an isomorphism

(45)

\begin{CD}CC\colon CF(X)@>{\sim}>{}>L(T^{*}M|_{X})\>.\end{CD}

Let us now explain the choice of the sign
(-1)^{\dim S}
in the Definition
3.34
of the characteristic cycle
CC
.

The differentiable
C^{k}
-function germ
f\colon(M,x)\to({\mathbb{R}},f(x))
(with
2\leq k\leq\infty
) has a
stratified Morse critical point
at
x\in S
, for a stratum
S\in{\mathscr{S}}
,
if and only if (see, e.g.,
[
67
, p.311]
or
[
113
, p.286]
):

(46)

\begin{cases}&df_{x}\in\left(T^{*}_{S}M\right)^{\circ}\;,\quad\text{i.e., $df_{x}\in T^{*}_{S}M$ is non-degenerate, and}\\
&\text{the graph $df(M)\subset T^{*}M$ of $df$ intersects $T_{S}^{*}M$
{\it transversally} at $df_{x}\,$.}\end{cases}

Using the
complex orientations
of
T^{*}M
and
M\simeq df(M)
one gets in this case for the corresponding

local intersection number
:

(47)

(-1)^{\dim S}\cdot\sharp_{df_{x}}\bigl(\,[T_{S}^{*}M]\cap[df(M)]\,\bigr)=(-1)^{\lambda}\>,

with
\lambda
the
Morse index
of
f|_{S}
at
x

(i.e., its Hessian at
x
has exactly
\lambda
negative eigenvalues).
Here
[df(M)]\in H^{BM}_{2m}(df(M);{\mathbb{Z}})
is the fundamental class of the oriented manifold
df(M)\simeq M
.

The local intersection number is defined similarly to the following
global
intersection number

\sharp\colon\>H^{BM}_{2m}(A;{\mathbb{Z}})\times H^{BM}_{2m}(B;{\mathbb{Z}})\to{\mathbb{Z}}

for two
closed
subsets
A,B\subset T^{*}M
with
A\cap B

compact
:

\begin{CD}H^{2m}_{A}(T^{*}M;{\mathbb{Z}})\times H^{2m}_{B}(T^{*}M;{\mathbb{Z}})@>{\cup}>{}>H^{4m}_{c}(T^{*}M;{\mathbb{Z}})@>{tr}>{}>{\mathbb{Z}}\\
@V{PD}V{\wr}V@V{PD}V{\wr}V\Big\|\\
H^{BM}_{2m}(A;{\mathbb{Z}})\times H^{BM}_{2m}(B;{\mathbb{Z}})@>{\cap}>{}>H_{0}(T^{*}M;{\mathbb{Z}})@>{deg}>{}>{\mathbb{Z}}\end{CD}

Here,
PD
is
Poincaré duality

given by the cap-product with the
fundamental class
[T^{*}M]
of the complex manifold
T^{*}M
.

Example 3.37
.

Let
g\colon(M,x)\to({\mathbb{C}},g(x))
be a holomorphic function germ so that the graph
dg(M)\subset T^{*}M
of
dg
intersects
T_{S}^{*}M

transversally
at
dg_{x}\,
. Then

\sharp_{dg_{x}}\bigl(\,[T_{S}^{*}M]\cap[dg(M)]\,\bigr)=1

and
g|_{S}
has a
complex Morse
critical point at
x\in S
. But if we identify the complex cotangent bundle
T^{*}M
with the real cotangent bundle of the underlying real (oriented) manifold, then the graph
dg(M)
of
g
gets identified with the graph
df(M)
of the real part
f:=Re(g)
of
g
.
So, with the complex orientations, one also has

\sharp_{df_{x}}\bigl(\,[T_{S}^{*}M]\cap[df(M)]\,\bigr)=1\>,

but
f|_{S}
has at
x\in S
a classical Morse critical point of index
\lambda=\dim S
.

The intersection theory in the ambient cotangent bundle
T^{*}M

is used for the formulation of the following beautiful
intersection formula

(see, e.g.,
[
113
, Theorem 5.0.4]
for a more general version).

Theorem 3.38
.

Let
f\colon M\to[a,d[\,\subset{\mathbb{R}}
be a
C^{\infty}
-function
(
a\leq d\leq\infty
),
with
f|_{X}
proper.
Suppose that
T^{*}_{{\mathscr{S}}}M\cap df(M)
is compact,
with
T^{*}_{{\mathscr{S}}}M
the union of conormal spaces
T_{S}^{*}M
to the strata
S\in{\mathscr{S}}
of the Whitney stratification
{\mathscr{S}}
of
X\hookrightarrow M
.
Then we have for all
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{{\mathscr{S}}-c}(X;R)
:

\displaystyle\dim_{R}H^{*}(X,{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\infty\>\text{, with}

(48)

\displaystyle\chi(X,{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sharp(\>CC(\chi_{stalk}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}))\cap[df(M)]\>)\>,

and

\displaystyle\dim_{R}H^{*}_{c}(X,{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\infty\>\text{, with}

(49)

\displaystyle\chi_{c}(X,{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sharp(\>CC(\chi_{stalk}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}))\cap[-df(M)]\>).

Proof.

The reader should compare the following proof also with the proof of Theorem
3.19
.
By approximation
[
8
]
, we can assume that
f
has only
stratified Morse critical points

with respect to
{\mathscr{S}}
, since the corresponding intersection number does not change
by a homotopy argument.
By assumption,
T^{*}_{{\mathscr{S}}}M\cap df(M)
is compact so that
f
has only finitely
many stratified Morse critical points. Choose
b\in[a,d[\,
with all these
critical points contained in
\{a\leq f<b\}
. Then one gets by Lemma
3.3

R\Gamma(X\cap\{f\leq b\},{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\simeq R\Gamma(X,{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\>,

and

R\Gamma_{c}(X\cap\{f<b\},{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\simeq R\Gamma_{c}(X,{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\>.

And these complexes belong to
D^{b}_{c}(\{pt\};R)
by Proposition
3.21
.
Similarly, the normal Morse data
NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},S)\in D^{b}_{c}(\{pt\};R)
by Corollary
3.25
.
Since the Euler characteristic
\chi
is additive, by Corollary
3.7

and Lemma
3.8
it is enough
to show that for such a stratified Morse critical point
x\in S
:

\chi(LMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},\pm f,x))=\sharp_{\pm\,df_{x}}\,(\>CC({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\cap[\pm\,df(M)]\>)\>.

But this follows from Theorem
3.12
:

LMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},\pm f,x)\simeq NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},S)[-\tau]\>,

with
\tau
the Morse index of
\pm f|_{S}
at
x
.
Finally, by the definition of
CC({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})
and Equation (
47
) we have

\displaystyle\chi(LMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},\pm f,x))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{\tau}\cdot\chi(NMD({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet},S))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sharp_{\pm\,df_{x}}\,(\>CC({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})\cap[\pm\,df(M)]\>)\>,

which completes the proof.
∎

This intersection Theorem
3.38
goes back
to Dubson
[
36
]
, Sabbah
[
107
]

and Ginsburg
[
43
]
(partially in the context of
holonomic
D
-modules).

Note that the condition that
T^{*}_{{\mathscr{S}}}M\cap df(M)
is compact just means that the stratified critical locus
\mathrm{Sing}_{{\mathscr{S}}}(f):=\bigcup_{S\in{\mathscr{S}}}\mathrm{Sing}(f|_{S})

is compact. The intersection formula above can also be reformulated in the language of constructible functions.

Assume
f\colon M\to[a,d[\,\subset{\mathbb{R}}
is a
C^{\infty}
-function
with
f|_{X}

proper
and
\pi\left(T^{*}_{{\mathscr{S}}}M\cap df(M)\right)\subset[a,b]
for some
b\in[a,d[\,
,
with
\pi
the projection
T^{*}M\to M
.
In particular,
T^{*}_{{\mathscr{S}}}M\cap df(M)
is
compact
.

Then one also has the following counterpart
of Theorem
3.38

for a constructible function
\alpha\in CF_{{\mathscr{S}}}(X)
:

(50)

\int_{X\cap\{f\leq r\}}\alpha\>d\chi=\sharp(\>CC(\alpha)\cap[df(M)]\>)\quad\text{for all $r\in\,]b,d[\,$,}

and

(51)

\int_{X\cap\{f<r\}}\alpha\>d\chi=\sharp(\>CC(\alpha)\cap[-df(M)]\>)\quad\text{for all $r\in\,]b,d[\,$.}

Note that
r\in\,]b,d[\,
is a stratified regular value of
f|_{X}
so that the left Euler characteristics are defined by Example
3.24
.
Let us give some examples, where the above conditions on
f
are satisfied.

Example 3.39

(Global index formula and Poincaré-Hopf theorem for singular spaces)
.

Let
X\hookrightarrow M
be a compact complex algebraic (or analytic) subvariety of
M
, with
{\mathscr{S}}
a given Whitney stratification of
X
.
Then we can take for
f
a constant function
so that
df(M)
is the zero-section.
Then we get for any
{\mathscr{S}}
-constructible function
\alpha\in CF_{{\mathscr{S}}}(X)
:

(52)

\int_{X}\,\alpha\>d\chi=\sharp(\>CC(\alpha)\cap[T^{*}_{M}M]\>)=\sharp(\>CC(\alpha)\cap[\omega(M)]\>)

for any differentiable one-form
\omega
on
M
, i.e., a section of
T^{*}M\to M
.
Assume that
T^{*}_{{\mathscr{S}}}M\cap\omega(M)
is finite, i.e., the set
\mathrm{Sing}_{{\mathscr{S}}}(\omega):=\bigcup_{S\in{\mathscr{S}}}\mathrm{Sing}(\omega|_{S})

of critical points of
\omega
with respect to
{\mathscr{S}}
is finite. If one defines for
x\in\mathrm{Sing}_{{\mathscr{S}}}(\omega)
and
\alpha\in CF_{{\mathscr{S}}}(X)
the

local index

ind_{x}(\omega,\alpha)
of
\omega
with respect to
\alpha
by

(53)

ind_{x}(\omega,\alpha):=\sharp_{\omega_{x}}\bigl(\,CC(\alpha)\cap[\omega(M)]\,\bigr)\>,

then one gets the Poincaré-Hopf theorem

(54)

\int_{X}\,\alpha\>d\chi=\sum_{x\in\mathrm{Sing}_{{\mathscr{S}}}(\omega)}\>ind_{x}(\omega,\alpha)\>.

See also
[
121
]
for an overview of different indices of vector fields and one forms in the context of singular complex varieties.
The following recent application is due to
[
4
]
(and implicitly already contained in
[
40
,
117
]
).

Example 3.40

(Effective characteristic cycles on an abelian variety)
.

Let
M=A
be a complex abelian variety, so that
A
is a projective abelian algebraic group with
trivial cotangent bundle
T^{*}A
. Let
\alpha\in CF(A)
be a constructible function with

CC(\alpha)
an
effective
cycle in
T^{*}A
.
By Kleiman’s transversality theorem, a
generic algebraic one-form

\omega
intersects
supp(CC(\alpha))
only in finitely many points

\omega_{x}
(see, e.g.,
[
117
, Proposition 2.8]
). Then the local intersection number (
53
) as well as the global
Euler characteristic (
53
) are non-negative:

(55)

ind_{x}(\omega,\alpha)=\sharp_{\omega_{x}}\bigl(\,CC(\alpha)\cap[\omega(A)]\,\bigr)\geq 0\quad\text{and}\quad\int_{A}\alpha\>d\chi\geq 0\>.

For example
\alpha
could be given by

(1)

\alpha=Eu_{Z}^{\vee}=(-1)^{\dim Z}\cdot Eu_{Z}
is the dual Euler obstruction of a pure dimensional closed subvariety
Z\hookrightarrow A

(see Equation (
59
) below).

(2)

\alpha=\chi_{stalk}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})
for a perverse sheaf
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in Perv(A;R)
.

(3)

\alpha=\chi_{stalk}(Rp_{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})
for a perverse sheaf
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in Perv(G;R)
on a
semi-abelian variety

G
, with
p\colon G\to A
the projection onto
the corresponding abelian variety
A
. Then

\chi(G;{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})=\chi(A;Rp_{*}{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})=\int_{A}\alpha\>d\chi\geq 0\>.

See
[
4
, Proposition 8.4, Example 8.5]
for a more general class of morphisms
p\colon G\to A
to an abelian variety with this property
for any perverse sheaf
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in Perv(G;R)

(for a different approach via
generic vanishing theorems
on semi-abelian varieties see (
128
) in Section
6
).

Example 3.41

(Affine varieties and global Euler obstruction)
.

Let
X\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{n}
be an
affine
complex algebraic variety endowed with a complex algebraic Whitney stratification
{\mathscr{S}}
.
Then the
semi-algebraic
distance function
r\colon{\mathbb{C}}^{n}\to[0,\infty[
,
r(z):=\sum_{i=1}^{n}z_{i}\cdot\bar{z}_{i}
has only finitely many critical values with respect to
{\mathscr{S}}
. So one gets for
{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{{\mathscr{S}}-c}(X;R)
:

\chi(X,{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})=\sharp(\>CC(\chi_{stalk}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}))\cap[dr({\mathbb{C}}^{n})]\>)\>,

and

\chi_{c}(X,{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})=\sharp(\>CC(\chi_{stalk}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}))\cap[-dr({\mathbb{C}}^{n})]\>)\>.

Similarly, for a constructible function
\alpha\in CF_{{\mathscr{S}}}(X)
:

\int_{X\cap\{r\leq\delta\}}\alpha\>d\chi=\sharp(\>CC(\alpha)\cap[dr({\mathbb{C}}^{n})]\>)\quad\text{for all $\delta>0$ large enough,}

and

\int_{X\cap\{r<\delta\}}\alpha\>d\chi=\sharp(\>CC(\alpha)\cap[-dr({\mathbb{C}}^{n})]\>)\quad\text{for all $\delta>0$ large enough.}

If
X
is pure dimensional and
CC(\alpha)=(-1)^{\dim X}\cdot\left[\overline{T^{*}_{X_{reg}}M}\right]
, then the intersection number

Eu(X):=(-1)^{\dim X}\cdot\sharp\left(\left[\overline{T^{*}_{X_{reg}}M}\right]\cap[dr({\mathbb{C}}^{n})]\>\right)

is by
[
113
, Equation (5.6.4)]
the
global Euler obstruction
of
X
in the sense of
Seade-Tibăr-Verjovsky
[
120
]

(and compare also with
[
36
, Theorem 1]
). The sign
(-1)^{\dim X}
comes from different orientation conventions.
As we will see in a moment, here
\alpha=Eu_{X}
is the
local Euler obstruction
function of

X
as defined by MacPherson
[
83
]
.

Example 3.42

(Local index formula)
.

Let
f\colon M\to[0,d[\;\subset{\mathbb{R}}
be a
real analytic
function, with
f|_{X}
proper. Then

f\circ\pi(\,T^{*}_{{\mathscr{S}}}M\cap df(M)\,)\subset[0,d[

is
subanalytic

and, by the
curve selection lemma
, it is
discrete
. Especially,

\pi(\,T^{*}_{{\mathscr{S}}}M\cap df(U)\,)\subset X\cap\{f=0\}\>,

if we restrict to
f\colon U:=\{f<\epsilon\}\to[0,\epsilon[\,
, with
0<\epsilon
small enough.
Then one gets for
\alpha\in CF_{{\mathscr{S}}}(X)
and
0<r<\epsilon
:

(56)

\int_{X\cap\{f=0\}}\,\alpha\>d\chi=\int_{X\cap\{f\leq r\}}\alpha\>d\chi=\sharp(\>CC(\alpha)\cap[df(U)]\>)\>.

The most important special case is the local situation

(M,x)\simeq({\mathbb{C}}^{n},0)
with
f(z):=r(z):=\sum_{i=1}^{n}z_{i}\cdot\bar{z}_{i}
. In this case we get for
0<\epsilon\ll 1
:

(57)

\alpha(x)=\sharp_{dr_{x}}(\>CC(\alpha)\cap[dr(\{r<\epsilon\})]\>)\>.

In particular, this local intersection number is independent of the choice of
the real analytic function
r
with
\{r=0\}=\{x\}
.
Moreover, it defines the inverse
of the characteristic cycle map (see, e.g.,
[
113
, Corollary 5.0.1]
and compare with
[
43
, Theorem 11.7]
).

Corollary 3.43
.

The inverse of the characteristic cycle map

CC\colon CF_{{\mathscr{S}}}(X)\to L({\mathscr{S}};T^{*}M)

is given by

Eu^{\vee}\colon L({\mathscr{S}};T^{*}M)\to CF_{{\mathscr{S}}}(X);

[C]\mapsto\alpha\;,\quad\text{with}\>\>\alpha(x):=\sharp_{dr_{x}}(\>[C]\cap[dr(\{r<\epsilon\})]\>)\>,

for
r,\epsilon
as above. In particular, for
CC(\alpha)=\sum_{S\in{\mathscr{S}}}m(S)\cdot\left[\overline{T^{*}_{S}M}\right]
, we get for
x\in X
:

(58)

\alpha(x)=\sum_{S\in{\mathscr{S}}}m(S)\cdot Eu^{\vee}\left(\left[\overline{T^{*}_{S}M}\right]\right)\>.

Note that this corollary is also a
refinement
of
[
43
, Theorem 11.7]
,
since we work with a
fixed stratification
. Assume now that
X\hookrightarrow M
is pure dimensional,
so that

C=\left[T^{*}_{X}M\right]:=\left[\overline{T^{*}_{X_{reg}}M}\right]\in L({\mathscr{S}};T^{*}M)

defines a corresponding Lagrangian cycle. Then one gets by
[
113
, Equation (5.35) and p.323-324]
that

(59)

Eu^{\vee}\left(\left[T^{*}_{X}M\right]\right)=Eu_{X}^{\vee}:=(-1)^{\dim X}\cdot Eu_{X}\in CF_{{\mathscr{S}}}(X)

is the
dual local Euler obstruction
function
Eu_{X}^{\vee}
, with
Eu_{X}
the famous
local Euler obstruction
function of

X
as defined by MacPherson
[
83
]
(compare also with
[
43
, Theorem 11.7]
).
The sign
(-1)^{\dim X}
comes again from different orientation conventions.
In particular, we get without any calculation
that the
local Euler obstruction

Eu_{X}
is constructible with respect
to
any Whitney stratification
of
X
.
Since this is a local result, it is then also true for any pure-dimensional
complex algebraic (or analytic) variety
X
(without any embedding into a complex manifold).
This result is due to Dubson
[
34
, Proposition 1, Theorem 3]

and Brasselet-Schwartz
[
13
, p.125, Corollary 10.2]
. Then the
local index formula
(
58
) can be reformulated as

(60)

\alpha(x)=\sum_{S\in{\mathscr{S}}}m(S)\cdot(-1)^{\dim S}Eu_{\bar{S}}(x)\quad\text{for $x\in X$}

and
CC(\alpha)=\sum_{S\in{\mathscr{S}}}m(S)\cdot\left[\overline{T^{*}_{S}M}\right]
for a given
\alpha\in CF_{{\mathscr{S}}}(X)
.

Remark 3.44
.

The
local index formula
(
60
)
goes back to a corresponding

index formula
of Kashiwara
[
62
]
(compare with
[
64
, Theorem 6.3.1]
)
for the
solution complex

Sol_{M}({\mathscr{M}}):=Rhom_{{\mathscr{D}}_{M}}({\mathscr{M}},{\mathscr{O}}_{M})
of a
holonomic

{\mathscr{D}}_{M}
-module on the complex
manifold
M
. Note that this solution complex is a complex analytically (or algebraically)
constructible complex of sheaves of
{\mathbb{C}}
-vector spaces, with finite dimensional
stalks (
[
67
, Theorem 11.3.7]
). Kashiwara’s formula corresponds to (
60
)
for the constructible function

\alpha:=\chi_{stalk}(\,Rhom_{{\mathscr{D}}_{M}}({\mathscr{M}},{\mathscr{O}}_{M})[\dim M]\,)\>.

Moreover, he works directly with the
characteristic cycle

of a holonomic
{\mathscr{D}}_{M}
-module.
The corresponding multiplicities of characteristic cycles fit by
[
113
, Example 5.3.4]
with our conventions.
Similarly, Kashiwara introduced for his index formula some topological invariants,
which are nothing else but the
Euler obstructions
of the closures
\bar{S}
for the strata
S\in{\mathscr{S}}
of a Whitney stratification
{\mathscr{S}}
of
M
so that
the characteristic variety
char({\mathscr{M}})\subset T^{*}_{{\mathscr{S}}}M
.
But this fact was only observed later on by Dubson (compare with
[
15
]
, and also with

[
64
, Introduction, p.xiii]
).

Other references for these formulae are
[
36
]
,
[
14
, p.545]
and
[
43
, Theorem 8.2, Theorem 11.7, p.393]

(but with an incorrect sign in
[
43
, Corollary 6.19(b) and Theorem 8.2]
).

So one gets in the complex analytic (or algebraic) context a commutative diagram (see also
[
43
]
):

(61)

\begin{CD}K_{0}(\text{holonomic ${\mathscr{D}}_{M}$-modules})@>{CC}>{}>L(T^{*}M)\\
@V{DR_{M}}V{Sol_{M}[\dim M]}V@A{\wr}A{CC}A\\
K_{0}(D^{b}_{c}(M;{\mathbb{C}}))@>{\chi_{stalk}}>{}>CF(M)\>.\end{CD}

Here
DR_{M}({\mathscr{M}})\simeq\,Rhom_{{\mathscr{D}}_{M}}({\mathscr{O}}_{M},{\mathscr{M}})[\dim M]
is the
De Rham complex
of the holonomic
{\mathscr{D}}_{M}
-module
{\mathscr{M}}
.
In the algebraic context one has of course to use the De Rham and Solution complex of the associated analytic
{\mathscr{D}}_{M}
-module.
That
DR_{M}({\mathscr{M}})
and
Sol_{M}({\mathscr{M}})
for a holonomic
{\mathscr{D}}_{M}
-module
{\mathscr{M}}
have the same characteristic cycle follows, e.g., from the fact that they are exchanged by the

duality
of holonomic
{\mathscr{D}}_{M}
-modules, resp.,
Verdier duality
for constructible sheaf complexes
(see, e.g.,
[
58
, Corollary 4.6.5]
). Finally, also the left vertical arrows become isomorphisms by the famous
Riemann-Hilbert correspondence

(see, e.g.,
[
58
, Theorem 7.2.1]
), if we restrict ourselves to

regular holonomic

{\mathscr{D}}_{M}
-modules.

3.4.
Functorial calculus of characteristic cycles

We continue to work in an embedded context and discuss the functorial calculus of characteristic cycles (see also, e.g.,
[
43
,
107
]
and compare with
[
67
, Chapter IX]
for counterparts in real geometry).
We explain the translation
into the context of Lagrangian cycles of the following operations for
constructible functions and sheaves: external product, proper direct image, non-characteristic pullback and specialization (i.e., nearby cycles),
together with an intersection formula for
vanishing cycles.

Note that by Corollary
3.22
and Corollary
3.25
, the constructible complexes

{\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet}\in D^{b}_{({\mathscr{S}}-)c}(X;R)\quad\text{ with}\quad\chi_{stalk}({\mathscr{F}}^{\bullet})=0\in CF_{({\mathscr{S}})}(X)

are
preserved
by all Grothendieck functors like pullback
f^{*}
, direct image
Rf_{!}=Rf_{*}
for a proper morphism, and the (external) tensor products

\otimes
resp.,
\boxtimes
(which are exact in the case
R
a field as considered here). So they induce similar transformations of constructible functions
CF_{({\mathscr{S}})}(X)
via the surjection

\chi_{stalk}\colon\>K_{0}\left(D^{b}_{({\mathscr{S}})-c}(X;R)\right)\to CF_{({\mathscr{S}})}(X)\>.

Similarly for the nearby and vanishing cycles functors
\psi_{f},\varphi_{f}
of Deligne, as studied in Section
4
. So it is natural to ask for a direct description of the corresponding transformations of characteristic cycles in the embedded context, compatible with the isomorphisms

\begin{CD}CC\colon CF_{{\mathscr{S}}}(X)@>{\sim}>{}>L({\mathscr{S}},T^{*}M)\quad\text{and}\quad CC\colon CF(X)@>{\sim}>{}>L(T^{*}M|_{X})\>.\end{CD}

Whenever possible, we formulate the corresponding results for the refined context of fixed Whitney stratifications.

Example 3.45

(Characteristic cycle of external products)
.

Let
X_{i}\hookrightarrow M_{i}
be a closed embedding of the complex algebraic (or analytic) variety
X_{i}
into a complex algebraic (or analytic) manifold
M_{i}
(for
i=1,2
). Assume
{\mathscr{S}}_{i}
is a Whitney stratification of
X_{i}
, with
\alpha_{i}\in CF_{{\mathscr{S}}_{i}}(X_{i})
(for
i=1,2
).
Then
\alpha_{1}\boxtimes\alpha_{2}\in CF_{{\mathscr{S}}_{1}\times{\mathscr{S}}_{2}}(X_{1}\times X_{2})
is defined by

\alpha_{1}\boxtimes\alpha_{2}(x_{1},x_{2}):=\alpha_{1}(x_{1})\cdot\alpha_{2}(x_{2})\quad\text{for $(x_{1},x_{2})\in X_{1}\times X_{2}$,}

with

(62)

CC(\alpha_{1}\boxtimes\alpha_{2})=CC(\alpha_{1})\boxtimes CC(\alpha_{2})\in L({\mathscr{S}}_{1}\times{\mathscr{S}}_{2},T^{*}M_{1}\times T^{*}M_{2})\>.

Here, we use the identification
T^{*}(M_{1}\times M_{2})=T^{*}M_{1}\times T^{*}M_{2}
, and this result follows directly from
the
product formula for normal Morse data
as in Example
3.17
.
Alternatively, it can be deduced from the

multiplicativity of the local Euler obstruction functions
(as stated in
[
83
, p. 426]
):

Eu_{X_{1}\times X_{2}}=Eu_{X_{1}}\boxtimes Eu_{X_{2}}

in case the
X_{i}
are pure-dimensional (
i=1,2
).

To formulate the results for suitable pullbacks
f^{*}
and direct images
f_{*}
for a morphism
f\colon M\to N
of complex (algebraic) manifolds,
we need to compare the corresponding cotangent bundles as in the following commutative diagram (whose right square is cartesian):

(63)

\begin{CD}T^{*}M@<{{}^{t}f^{\prime}}<{}<f^{*}(T^{*}N)@>{f_{\pi}}>{}>T^{*}N\\
@V{}V{\pi_{M}}V@V{}V{\pi}V@V{}V{\pi_{N}}V\\
M=M@>{f}>{}>N\>.\end{CD}

Here
{}^{t}f^{\prime}
is the dual of the differential of
f
, with
f_{\pi}
induced by base change.
We first consider the case of direct images. Let
X\hookrightarrow M
, resp.,
Y\hookrightarrow N
be closed complex algebraic (or analytic) subvarieties endowed with Whitney stratifications
{\mathscr{S}}
of
X
, resp.,
{\mathscr{T}}
of
Y
in such a way that
f\colon X\to Y
is a

proper stratified submersion
.
Then also
f_{\pi}\colon f^{*}(T^{*}N)|_{X}\to T^{*}N|_{Y}
is
proper
by base change. Moreover

f_{\pi}\left({}^{t}f^{\prime-1}\left(T^{*}_{{\mathscr{S}}}M\right)\right)\subset T^{*}_{{\mathscr{T}}}Y\>,

since
f\colon X\to Y
is a stratified submersion. So on the level of
Lagrangian cycles
one gets an induced group homomorphism

(64)

{f_{\pi*}}^{t}f^{\prime*}\colon L({\mathscr{S}},T^{*}M)\to L({\mathscr{T}},T^{*}N)\>,

with the pullback
{}^{t}f^{\prime*}
defined via Poincaré duality:

\begin{CD}H^{2m}_{T^{*}_{{\mathscr{S}}}M}(T^{*}M)@>{{}^{t}f^{\prime*}}>{}>H^{2m}_{{}^{t}f^{\prime-1}(T^{*}_{{\mathscr{S}}}M)}(f^{*}(T^{*}N),{\mathbb{Z}})\\
@V{PD}V{\wr}V@V{PD}V{\wr}V\\
H^{BM}_{2m}(T^{*}_{{\mathscr{S}}}M,{\mathbb{Z}})@>{{}^{t}f^{\prime*}}>{}>H^{BM}_{2n}(^{t}f^{\prime-1}(T^{*}_{{\mathscr{S}}}M),{\mathbb{Z}})\>,\end{CD}

with
m=\dim M,n=\dim N
and
T^{*}M,T^{*}N,f^{*}(T^{*}N)
oriented as complex manifolds.

Similarly, the pushforward of constructible functions fits into a commutative diagram

\begin{CD}K_{0}\left(D^{b}_{{\mathscr{S}}-c}(X;R)\right)@>{f_{*}}>{}>K_{0}\left(D^{b}_{{\mathscr{T}}-c}(Y;R)\right)\\
@V{\chi_{stalk}}V{}V@ VV\chi_{stalk}V\\
CF_{{\mathscr{S}}}(X)@>{f_{*}}>{}>CF_{{\mathscr{T}}}(Y)\>,\end{CD}

with

(65)

f_{*}(\alpha)(y):=\int_{X\cap\{f=y\}}\alpha d\chi\quad\text{for $y\in Y$ and $\alpha\in CF_{{\mathscr{S}}}(X)$.}

And these pushforwards of Lagrangian cycles and constructible functions are compatible with the characteristic cycle map
(see, e.g.,
[
115
, Section 4.6]
for the following proof).

Proposition 3.46
.

Let
\alpha\in CF_{{\mathscr{S}}}(X)
be given. Then

CC(f_{*}\alpha)={f_{\pi*}}^{t}f^{\prime*}CC(\alpha)\in L({\mathscr{T}},T^{*}N)\>.

Proof.

Let
\beta\in CF_{{\mathscr{T}}}(Y)
be defined by
CC(\beta)={f_{\pi*}}^{t}f^{\prime*}CC(\alpha)\in L({\mathscr{T}},T^{*}N)
.
Then we need to show
\beta=f_{*}\alpha
. For this we calculate
\beta(y)

for
y\in Y
as in Corollary
3.43
via

\beta(y):=\sharp_{dr_{y}}(\>{f_{\pi*}}^{t}f^{\prime*}CC(\alpha)\cap[dr(\{r<\epsilon\})]\>)\>,

with
r\colon(N,y)\simeq({\mathbb{C}}^{n},0)\to[0,\infty[\;
given by
r(z)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}z_{i}\cdot\bar{z}_{i}
the distance function to
y
(in local coordinates) and

0<\epsilon
small enough. By the
projection formula
one gets

\beta(y)=\sharp(\>CC(\alpha)\cap[d(r\circ f)(\{r\circ f<\epsilon\})]\>)\>.

And the last intersection number is by the
local index formula
(
56
) given by

\sharp(\>CC(\alpha)\cap[d(r\circ f)(\{r\circ f<\epsilon\})]\>)=\int_{X\cap\{f=y\}}\,\alpha\>d\chi=f_{*}\alpha(y)\>,

thus completing the proof.
∎

Example 3.47
.

Let
Y=N=\{pt\}
be a point with
f
a constant map, so that
f^{*}(T^{*}N)=T^{*}_{M}M
is the zero section of
T^{*}M
.
Then we recover the
global index formula
(
52
):

\int_{X}\,\alpha\>d\chi=\sharp(\>CC(\alpha)\cap[T^{*}_{M}M]\>)\>.

To define similarly a pullback of Lagrangian cycles, we have to go in diagram (
63
) into the opposite direction, and need the following properness condition for
{}^{t}f^{\prime}\colon f^{*}(T^{*}N)\to T^{*}M
(see, e.g.,
[
113
, Lemma 4.3.1]
).

Definition 3.48
.

Let
C\subset T^{*}N
be a closed
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-conic subset. Then
f\colon M\to N
is
non-characteristic
with respect to
C
, if one of the following two equivalent conditions holds:

(1)

f_{\pi}^{-1}(C)\cap kern(^{t}f^{\prime})\subset f^{*}(T^{*}_{N}N)
,
with
kern(^{t}f^{\prime})
the corresponding kernel bundle, and
f^{*}(T^{*}_{N}N)

the zero-section of
f^{*}(T^{*}N)
.

(2)

The map
\,{}^{t}f^{\prime}\colon f_{\pi}^{-1}(C)\to T^{*}M
is
proper
